The Dedukti translation drops implicit and instance arguments and maps Lean's arithmetic and logic to Informath's base (mapping.tsv); the other Mathlib constants are worded by the symbol table mathlib.dkgf, whose English wordings Informath also renders in the other languages. def Pi__le_def : (x : Set) -> (x1 : (Elem x -> Set)) -> (_ : Proof ((i3 : Elem x) -> LE (x1 i3))) -> (x3 : ((i2 : Elem x) -> x1 i2)) -> (y : ((i1 : Elem x) -> x1 i1)) -> Proof (iff (Leq x3 y) (forall x (i => Leq (x3 i) (y i)))) :=
  x => x1 => x2 => x3 => y => Iff__rfl (Leq x3 y).
English
Let $x$ be a set. Let $x_{1}$ be a function from elements of $x$ to sets such that for all elements $i_{3}$ of $x$, $x_{1}$ applied to $i_{3}$ is ordered. Let $x_{3}$ be a function from elements $i_{2}$ of $x$ to elements of $x_{1}$ applied to $i_{2}$. Let $y$ be a function from elements $i_{1}$ of $x$ to elements of $x_{1}$ applied to $i_{1}$. Then $x_{3} \leq y$, if and only if for all elements $i$ of $x$, $x_{3} (i)\leq y (i)$.
Formula
$x \in \mathrm{Set} , x_{1} \in x \rightarrow \mathrm{Set} , \forall i_{3} \in x . \mathrm{LE} (x_{1} (i_{3})), x_{3} \in x \rightarrow x_{1} (i_{2}), y \in x \rightarrow x_{1} (i_{1})\vdash x_{3} \leq y \leftrightarrow (\forall i \in x . x_{3} (i)\leq y (i))$
Proof

IsTransinductiveUnbundled.lean:24
IsTrans X r means the binary relation r on X is transitive.
Lean
class IsTrans (α : Sort*) (r : α → α → Prop) : Prop where
Dedukti
1 lines
IsTrans : (x : Set) -> (r : (Elem x -> Elem x -> Prop)) -> Prop.
English
Let $x$ be a set. Let $r$ be a function from elements of $x$ and elements of $x$ to propositions. Then we can say that $r$ is transitive.
Formula
$x \in \mathrm{Set} , r \in x \rightarrow x \rightarrow \mathrm{Prop} \vdash \mathrm{IsTrans} (x , r)\ \mathsf{prop}$
IsTrans.mkconstructorUnbundled.lean:24
Lean
class IsTrans (α : Sort*) (r : α → α → Prop) : Prop where
Dedukti
1 lines
IsTrans__mk : (x : Set) -> (r : (Elem x -> Elem x -> Prop)) -> (trans : Proof (forall x (a => forall x (b => forall x (c => if (r a b) (if (r b c) (r a c))))))) -> Proof (IsTrans x r).
English
Let $x$ be a set. Let $r$ be a function from elements of $x$ and elements of $x$ to propositions such that for all elements $a$, $b$ and $c$ of $x$, if $r$ holds for $a$ and $b$, then if $r$ holds for $b$ and $c$, then $r$ holds for $a$ and $c$. Then $r$ is transitive.
Formula
$x \in \mathrm{Set} , r \in x \rightarrow x \rightarrow \mathrm{Prop} , \forall a \in x . \forall b \in x . \forall c \in x . r (a , b)\rightarrow r (b , c)\rightarrow r (a , c)\vdash \mathrm{IsTrans} (x , r)$

IsTrans__trans : (x : Set) -> (r : (Elem x -> Elem x -> Prop)) -> (_ : Proof (IsTrans x r)) -> (a : Elem x) -> (b : Elem x) -> (c : Elem x) -> (h : Proof (r a b)) -> (h1 : Proof (r b c)) -> Proof (r a c).
English
Let $x$ be a set. Let $r$ be a transitive function from elements of $x$ and elements of $x$ to propositions. Let $a , b , c \in x$ such that $r$ holds for $a$ and $b$ and $r$ holds for $b$ and $c$. Then $r$ holds for $a$ and $c$.
Formula
$x \in \mathrm{Set} , r \in x \rightarrow x \rightarrow \mathrm{Prop} , \mathrm{IsTrans} (x , r), a , b , c \in x , r (a , b), r (b , c)\vdash r (a , c)$

theorem IsEquiv.of_equivalence {α : Sort*} {lt : α → α → Prop} (h : Equivalence lt) :
    IsEquiv α lt where
  refl := h.refl; symm _ _ := h.symm; trans _ _ _ := h.trans

theorem equivalence_iff_isEquiv {α : Sort*} (lt : α → α → Prop) : Equivalence lt ↔ IsEquiv α lt :=
  ⟨.of_equivalence, fun _ => .of_isEquiv lt⟩

def equivalence_iff_isEquiv : (x : Set) -> (lt : (Elem x -> Elem x -> Prop)) -> Proof (iff (Equivalence lt) (IsEquiv x lt)) :=
  x => lt => iffI (Equivalence lt) (IsEquiv x lt) (IsEquiv__of_equivalence x lt) (h => Equivalence__of_isEquiv x lt).
English

Mathlib.Order.Defs.PartialOrder source

PreorderinductivePartialOrder.lean:43
A preorder is a reflexive, transitive relation ≤. In a preorder, a < b means a ≤ b ∧ ¬b ≤ a, and < is defined this way by default. You can override this definition to set a better def-eq.
Lean
class Preorder (α : Type*) extends LE α, LT α where
Dedukti
1 lines
Preorder__structure : (G : Set) -> ({|≤|} : Rel G) -> (le_refl : Proof (forall G (a => appRel G {|≤|} a a))) -> (le_trans : Proof (forall G (a => forall G (b => forall G (c => if (appRel G {|≤|} a b) (if (appRel G {|≤|} b c) (appRel G {|≤|} a c))))))) -> Structure.
English
A preordered set is an algebraic structure, where
  • $G$ is a set.
  • $≤$ is a binary relation on $G$.
  • for all elements $a$ of $G$, $\appRelMACRoCD {≤}{a}{a}$.
  • for all elements $a$, $b$ and $c$ of $G$, if $\appRelMACRoCD {≤}{a}{b}$, then if $\appRelMACRoCD {≤}{b}{c}$, then $\appRelMACRoCD {≤}{a}{c}$.
Formula
$G \in \mathrm{Set} , ≤ \in \mathrm{Rel} (G), \forall a \in G . \appRelMACRoCM {≤}{a}{a}, \forall a \in G . \forall b \in G . \forall c \in G . \appRelMACRoCM {≤}{a}{b}\rightarrow \appRelMACRoCM {≤}{b}{c}\rightarrow \appRelMACRoCM {≤}{a}{c}\vdash \mathrm{Structure}$

tacticCompareOfLessAndEq_rfldefLinearOrder.lean:53
This attempts to prove that a given instance of compare is equal to compareOfLessAndEq by introducing the arguments and trying the following approaches in order:
1. seeing if rfl works 2. seeing if the compare at hand is nonetheless essentially compareOfLessAndEq, but, because of implicit arguments, requires us to unfold the defs and split the ifs in the definition of compareOfLessAndEq 3. seeing if we can split by cases on the arguments, then see if the defs work themselves out (useful when compare is defined via a match statement, as it is for Bool)
Lean
macro "compareOfLessAndEq_rfl" : tactic =>
  `(tactic| (intro a b; first | rfl |
    (simp only [compare, compareOfLessAndEq]; split_ifs <;> rfl) |
    (induction a <;> induction b <;> simp +decide only)))
Dedukti
1 lines
tacticCompareOfLessAndEq_rfl : Elem Lean__ParserDescr.
English
$\mathrm{tacticCompareOfLessAndEq\_rfl}$ is an element of $\operatorname{ParserDescr}$.
Formula
$\mathrm{tacticCompareOfLessAndEq\_rfl} \in \operatorname{ParserDescr}$
LinearOrderinductiveLinearOrder.lean:60
A linear order is reflexive, transitive, antisymmetric and total relation ≤. We assume that every linear ordered type has decidable (≤), (<), and (=).

A linearly ordered set is a partially ordered set $G$ such that for all elements $a$ and $b$ of $G$, $a \leq b$ or $b \leq a$.

Mathlib.Order.Defs.Lattice source

SemilatticeSupinductiveLattice.lean:56
A SemilatticeSup is a join-semilattice, that is, a partial order with a join (a.k.a. lub / least upper bound, sup / supremum) operation ⊔ which is the least element larger than both factors.
Lean
class SemilatticeSup (α : Type u) extends PartialOrder α where
Dedukti
1 lines
SemilatticeSup__structure : (G : Set) -> (h : Proof (PartialOrder G)) -> ({|⊔|} : Oper G) -> (le_sup_left : Proof (forall G (a => forall G (b => Leq a (appOper G {|⊔|} a b))))) -> (le_sup_right : Proof (forall G (a => forall G (b => Leq b (appOper G {|⊔|} a b))))) -> (sup_le : Proof (forall G (a => forall G (b => forall G (c => if (Leq a c) (if (Leq b c) (Leq (appOper G {|⊔|} a b) c))))))) -> Structure.
English
A join semilattice is a partially ordered set $G$ with a binary operation $⊔$ on $G$ such that for all elements $a$ and $b$ of $G$, $a \leq \appOperMACRoCA {⊔}{a}{b}$ and $b \leq \appOperMACRoCA {⊔}{a}{b}$ and for all elements $a$, $b$ and $c$ of $G$, if $a \leq c$, then if $b \leq c$, then $\appOperMACRoCA {⊔}{a}{b}\leq c$.
Formula
$G \in \mathrm{Set} , \mathrm{PartialOrder} (G), ⊔ \in \mathrm{Oper} (G), \forall a \in G . \forall b \in G . a \leq \appOperMACRoCJ {⊔}{a}{b}, \forall a \in G . \forall b \in G . b \leq \appOperMACRoCJ {⊔}{a}{b}, \forall a \in G . \forall b \in G . \forall c \in G . a \leq c \rightarrow b \leq c \rightarrow \appOperMACRoCJ {⊔}{a}{b}\leq c \vdash \mathrm{Structure}$
SemilatticeSup.mkconstructorLattice.lean:56
Lean
class SemilatticeSup (α : Type u) extends PartialOrder α where
Dedukti
1 lines
SemilatticeSup__mk : (sup : (Elem x -> Elem x -> x)) -> (le_sup_left : Proof (forall x (a2 => forall x (b2 => Leq a2 (sup a2 b2))))) -> (le_sup_right : Proof (forall x (a1 => forall x (b1 => Leq b1 (sup a1 b1))))) -> (sup_le : Proof (forall x (a => forall x (b => forall x (c => if (Leq a c) (if (Leq b c) (Leq (sup a b) c))))))) -> Proof (SemilatticeSup x).
English
Let $\mathrm{sup}$ be a function from elements of $x$ and elements of $x$ to elements of $x$ such that for all elements $a_{2}$ and $b_{2}$ of $x$, $a_{2} \leq \mathrm{sup} (a_{2} , b_{2})$, for all elements $a_{1}$ and $b_{1}$ of $x$, $b_{1} \leq \mathrm{sup} (a_{1} , b_{1})$ and for all elements $a$, $b$ and $c$ of $x$, if $a \leq c$, then if $b \leq c$, then $\mathrm{sup} (a , b)\leq c$. Then $x$ is a join semilattice.
Formula
$\mathrm{sup} \in x \rightarrow x \rightarrow x , \forall a_{2} \in x . \forall b_{2} \in x . a_{2} \leq \mathrm{sup} (a_{2} , b_{2}), \forall a_{1} \in x . \forall b_{1} \in x . b_{1} \leq \mathrm{sup} (a_{1} , b_{1}), \forall a \in x . \forall b \in x . \forall c \in x . a \leq c \rightarrow b \leq c \rightarrow \mathrm{sup} (a , b)\leq c \vdash \mathrm{SemilatticeSup} (x)$

A SemilatticeInf is a meet-semilattice, that is, a partial order with a meet (a.k.a. glb / greatest lower bound, inf / infimum) operation ⊓ which is the greatest element smaller than both factors.
Lean
@[to_dual]
class SemilatticeInf (α : Type u) extends PartialOrder α where
Dedukti
1 lines
SemilatticeInf__structure : (G : Set) -> (h : Proof (PartialOrder G)) -> ({|⊓|} : Oper G) -> (inf_le_left : Proof (forall G (a => forall G (b => Leq (appOper G {|⊓|} a b) a)))) -> (inf_le_right : Proof (forall G (a => forall G (b => Leq (appOper G {|⊓|} a b) b)))) -> (le_inf : Proof (forall G (a => forall G (b => forall G (c => if (Leq a b) (if (Leq a c) (Leq a (appOper G {|⊓|} b c)))))))) -> Structure.
English
A meet semilattice is a partially ordered set $G$ with a binary operation $⊓$ on $G$ such that for all elements $a$ and $b$ of $G$, $\appOperMACRoCA {⊓}{a}{b}\leq a$ and $\appOperMACRoCA {⊓}{a}{b}\leq b$ and for all elements $a$, $b$ and $c$ of $G$, if $a \leq b$, then if $a \leq c$, then $a \leq \appOperMACRoCA {⊓}{b}{c}$.
Formula
$G \in \mathrm{Set} , \mathrm{PartialOrder} (G), ⊓ \in \mathrm{Oper} (G), \forall a \in G . \forall b \in G . \appOperMACRoCJ {⊓}{a}{b}\leq a , \forall a \in G . \forall b \in G . \appOperMACRoCJ {⊓}{a}{b}\leq b , \forall a \in G . \forall b \in G . \forall c \in G . a \leq b \rightarrow a \leq c \rightarrow a \leq \appOperMACRoCJ {⊓}{b}{c}\vdash \mathrm{Structure}$

class SemilatticeInf (α : Type u) extends PartialOrder α where
Dedukti
1 lines
SemilatticeInf__mk : (inf : (Elem x -> Elem x -> x)) -> (inf_le_left : Proof (forall x (a2 => forall x (b2 => Leq (inf a2 b2) a2)))) -> (inf_le_right : Proof (forall x (a1 => forall x (b1 => Leq (inf a1 b1) b1)))) -> (le_inf : Proof (forall x (a => forall x (b => forall x (c => if (Leq a b) (if (Leq a c) (Leq a (inf b c)))))))) -> Proof (SemilatticeInf x).
English
Let $\mathrm{inf}$ be a function from elements of $x$ and elements of $x$ to elements of $x$ such that for all elements $a_{2}$ and $b_{2}$ of $x$, $\mathrm{inf} (a_{2} , b_{2})\leq a_{2}$, for all elements $a_{1}$ and $b_{1}$ of $x$, $\mathrm{inf} (a_{1} , b_{1})\leq b_{1}$ and for all elements $a$, $b$ and $c$ of $x$, if $a \leq b$, then if $a \leq c$, then $a \leq \mathrm{inf} (b , c)$. Then $x$ is a meet semilattice.
Formula
$\mathrm{inf} \in x \rightarrow x \rightarrow x , \forall a_{2} \in x . \forall b_{2} \in x . \mathrm{inf} (a_{2} , b_{2})\leq a_{2} , \forall a_{1} \in x . \forall b_{1} \in x . \mathrm{inf} (a_{1} , b_{1})\leq b_{1} , \forall a \in x . \forall b \in x . \forall c \in x . a \leq b \rightarrow a \leq c \rightarrow a \leq \mathrm{inf} (b , c)\vdash \mathrm{SemilatticeInf} (x)$

@[to_dual (attr := gcongr)]
theorem sup_le_sup (h₁ : a ≤ b) (h₂ : c ≤ d) : a ⊔ c ≤ b ⊔ d :=
  sup_le (le_sup_of_le_left h₁) (le_sup_of_le_right h₂)

2 lines
def sup_le_sup : (x : Set) -> (_ : Proof (SemilatticeSup x)) -> (a : Elem x) -> (b : Elem x) -> (c : Elem x) -> (d : Elem x) -> (h : Proof (Leq a b)) -> (h1 : Proof (Leq c d)) -> Proof (Leq (Max__max a c) (Max__max b d)) :=
  x => x1 => a => b => c => d => h => h1 => sup_le x a c (Max__max b d) (le_sup_of_le_left x b d a h) (le_sup_of_le_right x b d c h1).

$x \in \mathrm{Set} , \mathrm{SemilatticeSup} (x), a , b , c , d \in x , a \leq b , c \leq d \vdash \MaxmaxMACRoBX {a}{c}\leq \MaxmaxMACRoBX {b}{d}$

4 lines

3.By le_sup_of_le_right, $c \leq \MaxmaxMACRoBX {b}{d}$.le_sup_of_le_right 1

class Lattice (α : Type u) extends SemilatticeSup α, SemilatticeInf α
Dedukti
1 lines
Lattice__structure : (G : Set) -> (h : Proof (SemilatticeSup G)) -> ({|⊓|} : Oper G) -> (inf_le_left : Proof (forall G (a => forall G (b => Leq (appOper G {|⊓|} a b) a)))) -> (inf_le_right : Proof (forall G (a => forall G (b => Leq (appOper G {|⊓|} a b) b)))) -> (le_inf : Proof (forall G (a => forall G (b => forall G (c => if (Leq a b) (if (Leq a c) (Leq a (appOper G {|⊓|} b c)))))))) -> Structure.
English
A lattice is a join semilattice $G$ with a binary operation $⊓$ on $G$ such that for all elements $a$ and $b$ of $G$, $\appOperMACRoCA {⊓}{a}{b}\leq a$ and $\appOperMACRoCA {⊓}{a}{b}\leq b$ and for all elements $a$, $b$ and $c$ of $G$, if $a \leq b$, then if $a \leq c$, then $a \leq \appOperMACRoCA {⊓}{b}{c}$.

DistribLatticeinductiveLattice.lean:294
A distributive lattice is a lattice that satisfies any of four equivalent distributive properties (of sup over inf or inf over sup, on the left or right).
The definition here chooses le_sup_inf: (x ⊔ y) ⊓ (x ⊔ z) ≤ x ⊔ (y ⊓ z). To prove distributivity from the dual law, use DistribLattice.ofInfSupLe.
A classic example of a distributive lattice is the lattice of subsets of a set, and in fact this example is generic in the sense that every distributive lattice is realizable as a sublattice of a powerset lattice.
Lean

English
A distributive lattice is a lattice $G$ such that for all elements $x$, $y$ and $z$ of $G$, $\latticeInfMACRoCF {(\latticeSupMACRoCE {x}{y})}{(\latticeSupMACRoCE {x}{z})}\leq \latticeSupMACRoCE {x}{(\latticeInfMACRoCF {y}{z})}$.

theorem inf_sup_left (a b c : α) : a ⊓ (b ⊔ c) = a ⊓ b ⊔ a ⊓ c :=
  calc
    a ⊓ (b ⊔ c) = a ⊓ (a ⊔ c) ⊓ (b ⊔ c) := by rw [inf_sup_self]
    _ = a ⊓ (a ⊓ b ⊔ c) := by simp only [inf_assoc, sup_inf_right]
    _ = (a ⊔ a ⊓ b) ⊓ (a ⊓ b ⊔ c) := by rw [sup_inf_self]
    _ = (a ⊓ b ⊔ a) ⊓ (a ⊓ b ⊔ c) := by rw [sup_comm]
    _ = a ⊓ b ⊔ a ⊓ c := by rw [sup_inf_left]

@[to_dual self (reorder := x y, h₁ h₂)]
theorem le_of_inf_le_sup_le (h₁ : x ⊓ z ≤ y ⊓ z) (h₂ : x ⊔ z ≤ y ⊔ z) : x ≤ y :=
  calc
    x ≤ y ⊓ z ⊔ x := le_sup_right
    _ = (y ⊔ x) ⊓ (x ⊔ z) := by rw [sup_inf_right, sup_comm x]
    _ ≤ (y ⊔ x) ⊓ (y ⊔ z) := inf_le_inf_left _ h₂
    _ = y ⊔ x ⊓ z := by rw [← sup_inf_left]
    _ ≤ y ⊔ y ⊓ z := sup_le_sup_left h₁ _
    _ ≤ _ := sup_le (le_refl y) inf_le_left

3.$\MaxmaxMACRoBX {b}{a}\leq \MaxmaxMACRoBX {c}{a}$.le_of_eq 1

5.$\EqtMACRoAU {\MinminMACRoBY {c}{a}}{\MinminMACRoBY {b}{a}}$.Eq.symm 1
6.$\MinminMACRoBY {c}{a}\leq \MinminMACRoBY {b}{a}$.le_of_eq 5
7.$\EqtMACRoAU {\MaxmaxMACRoBX {c}{a}}{\MaxmaxMACRoBX {b}{a}}$.Eq.symm 1
8.$\MaxmaxMACRoBX {c}{a}\leq \MaxmaxMACRoBX {b}{a}$.le_of_eq 7
9.By le_of_inf_le_sup_le, $c \leq b$.le_of_inf_le_sup_le 6, 8
10.$\EqtMACRoAU {b}{c}$.le_antisymm 4, 9
